I don’t really know how to separate it, honestly. During the day I’m busy enough that I don’t always think, “Oh, I’m anxious,” but I’m definitely tense a lot. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes. I mean… I don’t think of it as “avoiding” at first, because I tell myself I’m just prioritizing or I’ll get to it when I have a clearer head, but honestly, yes.

At work, the bigger models and presentations have become this thing I almost circle around. I’ll open the file, look at it, feel completely overwhelmed by how much could be wrong or how many assumptions someone might challenge, and then I’ll go answer emails or tweak some smaller thing that doesn’t matter as much. And then of course the important work slips, which makes everything worse. I actually got a formal performance warning recently because my models and presentations have been late or not where they need to be. That was… humiliating, honestly. I’ve always been someone people could rely on for that.

And outside work, I’ve basically stopped showing up for things that used to keep me steady. I used to volunteer teaching, and I had yoga, and a book club. They weren’t huge things, but they made my life feel like it had something in it besides spreadsheets and deadlines. Now I avoid them because the thought of having to be “on,” or answer people asking where I’ve been, or just manage another commitment feels like too much. So I don’t go, and then I feel worse because my life has gotten very small.

Yes… I have. I mean, I don’t always label it as anxiety in the moment, but that’s probably what it is. I’ve stopped going to things I used to actually care about — volunteer teaching, yoga, book club. Those were sort of the pieces of my life that made me feel like I wasn’t just work and exhaustion, you know? And now even the thought of showing up feels like too much.

Part of it is worrying that people will ask where I’ve been, or that I’ll have to explain myself, or just make conversation and seem normal. So I tell myself I’m too busy or I’ll go next week, but then I don’t. And it’s not helping — my life has gotten really narrow.

Work has been affected too. I’ve had a formal performance warning because my models and presentations keep slipping, and I think some of that is the same pattern — avoiding things that feel too overwhelming, then falling further behind. It’s embarrassing to say out loud, because I used to be much more reliable than this.

I don’t always notice it until I realize my shoulders are up around my ears or I’ve been rereading the same line over and over because my mind is already ten steps ahead.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain tasks or situations because they make you feel anxious or overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… I don’t think of it as “avoiding” at first, because I tell myself I’m just prioritizing or I’ll get to it when I have a clearer head, but honestly, yes.\n\nAt work, the bigger models and presentations have become this thing I almost circle around. I’ll open the file, look at it, feel completely overwhelmed by how much could be wrong or how many assumptions someone might challenge, and then I’ll go answer emails or tweak some smaller thing that doesn’t matter as much. And then of course the important work slips, which makes everything worse. I actually got a formal performance warning recently because my models and presentations have been late or not where they need to be. That was… humiliating, honestly. I’ve always been someone people could rely on for that.\n\nAnd outside work, I’ve basically stopped showing up for things that used to keep me steady. I used to volunteer teaching, and I had yoga, and a book club. They weren’t huge things, but they made my life feel like it had something in it besides spreadsheets and deadlines. Now I avoid them because the thought of having to be “on,” or answer people asking where I’ve been, or just manage another commitment feels like too much. So I don’t go, and then I feel worse because my life has gotten very small.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any physical symptoms like headaches or stomach problems during the day, not just when waking up in the morning?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, I don’t think I’ve been tracking it that clearly. The morning stomach-tightness stands out because it’s so immediate when I wake up, so I notice that. During the day, I’m often just pushing through whatever is happening, so I don’t always stop and think, “This is a physical symptom.”\n\nI wouldn’t want to say yes in a definite way if I’m not sure. I guess I notice tension in my body sometimes, but as far as actual headaches or stomach problems during the day… I don’t know. Nothing specific is jumping out at me right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social interactions or gatherings because you feel anxious about them?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. I mean, I don’t always label it as anxiety in the moment, but that’s probably what it is. I’ve stopped going to things I used to actually care about — volunteer teaching, yoga, book club. Those were sort of the pieces of my life that made me feel like I wasn’t just work and exhaustion, you know? And now even the thought of showing up feels like too much.\n\nPart of it is worrying that people will ask where I’ve been, or that I’ll have to explain myself, or just make conversation and seem normal. So I tell myself I’m too busy or I’ll go next week, but then I don’t. And it’s not helping — my life has gotten really narrow.\n\nWork has been affected too. I’ve had a formal performance warning because my models and presentations keep slipping, and I think some of that is the same pattern — avoiding things that feel too overwhelming, then falling further behind. It’s embarrassing to say out loud, because I used to be much more reliable than this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden feeling of intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think I’ve had something that clearly fits that, like a sudden wave of terror that peaks really fast and feels like an “attack,” if that’s what you mean.\n\nI do get overwhelmed, and sometimes it can feel like everything is too much, but it’s more… it builds, or it’s tied to a situation I’m already dreading. I’m not thinking of a specific time where out of nowhere I had intense fear or discomfort that shot up within minutes. At least nothing that stands out to me that way.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I mean, I definitely feel dulled sometimes, like my reactions are slower or flatter than they should be, but I don’t know if that’s the same as being disconnected from my thoughts or feelings.

My thoughts are usually very much there — almost too much there — but feelings can be harder to access or name, if that makes sense. Like I’ll know intellectually that something should make me happy or upset, and I can talk through it logically, but emotionally it feels muted or far away. But I still feel like they’re mine. I don’t feel separate from them in the way you might mean.

So… I don’t think I’ve noticed a true disconnection, exactly. More like I’m tired and kind of numbed out at times, but not detached from myself.

By the time I get to work, I feel like I never really rested at all. It’s just been wearing me down, and I didn’t know what else to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or worried during the day, outside of when you're lying awake at night?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to separate it, honestly. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes. Very much. I mean, that’s honestly one of the main reasons I came in — I barely sleep in any way that actually helps anymore.

I’ll go to bed exhausted, but then I just lie there awake, replaying work in my head… client models, numbers, things I need to fix, conversations I had or need to have. It’s like my brain won’t shut down. And then even if I do fall asleep, I’ll wake up at 3 or 4 in the morning with my stomach tight, already feeling like I’m behind before the day has even started.

So by the time I get to work, I feel like I never really rested. I’m physically there, but I feel worn out during the day, like I’m dragging myself through it.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t think so… not in the way you’re describing, like a sudden wave of terror out of nowhere.

I do get tense or uneasy sometimes, especially when I’m already worn down, but it usually feels tied to something I’m thinking about or dealing with. It’s not like I’m sitting there and then suddenly I’m overwhelmed by fear for no reason. I wouldn’t describe it as intense fear, exactly.

Maybe discomfort, yes, but more gradual, more like it builds or sits there in the background. I don’t think I’ve had those sudden episodes where it comes on sharply and unexpectedly.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I’m barely sleeping in any way that actually helps. I mean, I’m in bed, technically, but I’ll lie there for hours with my mind running through client models and numbers and assumptions, like I’m still at my desk, and I can’t seem to shut it off.\n\nAnd then even when I do fall asleep, I wake up at 3 or 4 in the morning with my stomach all tight, like I’m already bracing for the day. By the time I get to work, I feel like I never really rested at all. It’s just been wearing me down, and I didn’t know what else to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or worried during the day, outside of when you're lying awake at night?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to separate it, honestly. During the day I’m busy enough that I don’t always think, “Oh, I’m anxious,” but I’m definitely tense a lot. It’s like I’m constantly trying to keep everything from slipping, especially at work. I’ll be looking at one set of figures and already worrying about the next thing, or whether I missed something, or whether someone’s going to ask a question I should’ve anticipated.\n\nSo… yes, I guess there is worry during the day, but it feels almost normal to me at this point, if that makes sense. Like it’s just the background noise of my day. I don’t always notice it until I realize my shoulders are up around my ears or I’ve been rereading the same line over and over because my mind is already ten steps ahead.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain tasks or situations because they make you feel anxious or overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… I don’t think of it as “avoiding” at first, because I tell myself I’m just prioritizing or I’ll get to it when I have a clearer head, but honestly, yes.\n\nAt work, the bigger models and presentations have become this thing I almost circle around. I’ll open the file, look at it, feel completely overwhelmed by how much could be wrong or how many assumptions someone might challenge, and then I’ll go answer emails or tweak some smaller thing that doesn’t matter as much. And then of course the important work slips, which makes everything worse. I actually got a formal performance warning recently because my models and presentations have been late or not where they need to be. That was… humiliating, honestly. I’ve always been someone people could rely on for that.\n\nAnd outside work, I’ve basically stopped showing up for things that used to keep me steady. I used to volunteer teaching, and I had yoga, and a book club. They weren’t huge things, but they made my life feel like it had something in it besides spreadsheets and deadlines. Now I avoid them because the thought of having to be “on,” or answer people asking where I’ve been, or just manage another commitment feels like too much. So I don’t go, and then I feel worse because my life has gotten very small.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any physical symptoms like headaches or stomach problems during the day, not just when waking up in the morning?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, I don’t think I’ve been tracking it that clearly. The morning stomach-tightness stands out because it’s so immediate when I wake up, so I notice that. I guess I notice tension in my body sometimes, but as far as actual headaches or stomach problems during the day… I don’t know. Nothing specific is jumping out at me right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social interactions or gatherings because you feel anxious about them?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. I mean, I don’t always label it as anxiety in the moment, but that’s probably what it is. I’ve stopped going to things I used to actually care about — volunteer teaching, yoga, book club. Those were sort of the pieces of my life that made me feel like I wasn’t just work and exhaustion, you know? What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I’m barely sleeping in any way that actually helps. I mean, I’m in bed, technically, but I’ll lie there for hours with my mind running through client models and numbers and assumptions, like I’m still at my desk, and I can’t seem to shut it off.\n\nAnd then even when I do fall asleep, I wake up at 3 or 4 in the morning with my stomach all tight, like I’m already bracing for the day. By the time I get to work, I feel like I never really rested at all. It’s just been wearing me down, and I didn’t know what else to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or worried during the day, outside of when you're lying awake at night?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to separate it, honestly. During the day I’m busy enough that I don’t always think, “Oh, I’m anxious,” but I’m definitely tense a lot. It’s like I’m constantly trying to keep everything from slipping, especially at work. I’ll be looking at one set of figures and already worrying about the next thing, or whether I missed something, or whether someone’s going to ask a question I should’ve anticipated.\n\nSo… yes, I guess there is worry during the day, but it feels almost normal to me at this point, if that makes sense. Like it’s just the background noise of my day. I don’t always notice it until I realize my shoulders are up around my ears or I’ve been rereading the same line over and over because my mind is already ten steps ahead.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain tasks or situations because they make you feel anxious or overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… I don’t think of it as “avoiding” at first, because I tell myself I’m just prioritizing or I’ll get to it when I have a clearer head, but honestly, yes.\n\nAt work, the bigger models and presentations have become this thing I almost circle around. I’ll open the file, look at it, feel completely overwhelmed by how much could be wrong or how many assumptions someone might challenge, and then I’ll go answer emails or tweak some smaller thing that doesn’t matter as much. And then of course the important work slips, which makes everything worse. I actually got a formal performance warning recently because my models and presentations have been late or not where they need to be. That was… humiliating, honestly. I’ve always been someone people could rely on for that.\n\nAnd outside work, I’ve basically stopped showing up for things that used to keep me steady. I used to volunteer teaching, and I had yoga, and a book club. They weren’t huge things, but they made my life feel like it had something in it besides spreadsheets and deadlines. Now I avoid them because the thought of having to be “on,” or answer people asking where I’ve been, or just manage another commitment feels like too much. So I don’t go, and then I feel worse because my life has gotten very small.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any physical symptoms like headaches or stomach problems during the day, not just when waking up in the morning?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, I don’t think I’ve been tracking it that clearly. The morning stomach-tightness stands out because it’s so immediate when I wake up, so I notice that. During the day, I’m often just pushing through whatever is happening, so I don’t always stop and think, “This is a physical symptom.”\n\nI wouldn’t want to say yes in a definite way if I’m not sure. I guess I notice tension in my body sometimes, but as far as actual headaches or stomach problems during the day… I don’t know. Nothing specific is jumping out at me right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social interactions or gatherings because you feel anxious about them?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. I mean, I don’t always label it as anxiety in the moment, but that’s probably what it is. I’ve stopped going to things I used to actually care about — volunteer teaching, yoga, book club. Those were sort of the pieces of my life that made me feel like I wasn’t just work and exhaustion, you know? And now even the thought of showing up feels like too much.\n\nPart of it is worrying that people will ask where I’ve been, or that I’ll have to explain myself, or just make conversation and seem normal. So I tell myself I’m too busy or I’ll go next week, but then I don’t. And it’s not helping — my life has gotten really narrow.\n\nWork has been affected too. I’ve had a formal performance warning because my models and presentations keep slipping, and I think some of that is the same pattern — avoiding things that feel too overwhelming, then falling further behind. It’s embarrassing to say out loud, because I used to be much more reliable than this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden feeling of intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think I’ve had something that clearly fits that, like a sudden wave of terror that peaks really fast and feels like an “attack,” if that’s what you mean.\n\nI do get overwhelmed, and sometimes it can feel like everything is too much, but it’s more… it builds, or it’s tied to a situation I’m already dreading. I’m not thinking of a specific time where out of nowhere I had intense fear or discomfort that shot up within minutes. At least nothing that stands out to me that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're observing yourself from outside your body or like you're in a dream?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. Or at least… not in a clear way that I would describe like that. I know what you mean, I think — like feeling detached from yourself, or unreal, or like you’re watching yourself from somewhere else — and that’s not really something I’ve noticed.\n\nThere are times when I feel kind of numb or like I’m going through the motions, but I wouldn’t say I feel outside my body or like I’m in a dream. It’s more just… I’m there, I’m aware of what’s happening, I just may not feel very connected or engaged. But no, I can’t think of a time where I literally felt like I was observing myself from outside or that things around me weren’t real.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're disconnected from your thoughts or feelings?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. It’s like my brain won’t shut down. And then even if I do fall asleep, I’ll wake up at 3 or 4 in the morning with my stomach tight, already feeling like I’m behind before the day has even started.\n\nSo by the time I get to work, I feel like I never really rested. I’m physically there, but I feel worn out during the day, like I’m dragging myself through it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden, intense feeling of fear or discomfort that seemed to come out of nowhere?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re describing, like a sudden wave of terror out of nowhere.\n\nI do get tense or uneasy sometimes, especially when I’m already worn down, but it usually feels tied to something I’m thinking about or dealing with. It’s not like I’m sitting there and then suddenly I’m overwhelmed by fear for no reason. I wouldn’t describe it as intense fear, exactly.\n\nMaybe discomfort, yes, but more gradual, more like it builds or sits there in the background. I don’t think I’ve had those sudden episodes where it comes on sharply and unexpectedly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a panic attack?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I have, no. At least not what I understand a panic attack to be.\n\nI’ve had periods where I feel really uncomfortable or keyed up, but it’s not like this sudden, overwhelming rush where I feel like I’m losing control or something terrible is happening. It’s more… I don’t know, a steady tension, or being worn down and uneasy. If panic attack means something very specific, maybe I’m not describing it correctly, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had one.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I think they’re very much connected to work, yes. Not separate, or at least it doesn’t feel separate to me.

The fatigue is mostly because I’m not sleeping in any useful way anymore. I’ll get into bed already exhausted, and then instead of actually resting, I’m lying there going over client models, numbers, what I may have missed, what I need to fix the next morning. It just keeps looping. And then if I do fall asleep, I often wake up around 3 or 4 in the morning with my stomach tight, like my body has already started the workday before I’ve even opened my laptop.

So by the time I’m actually at work, I feel like I never really slept. It’s not just being tired like I had a busy week — it’s more like I never got to recover in the first place.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I’m barely sleeping in any way that actually helps. I mean, I’m in bed, technically, but I’ll lie there for hours with my mind running through client models and numbers and assumptions, like I’m still at my desk, and I can’t seem to shut it off.\n\nAnd then even when I do fall asleep, I wake up at 3 or 4 in the morning with my stomach all tight, like I’m already bracing for the day. By the time I get to work, I feel like I never really rested at all. It’s just been wearing me down, and I didn’t know what else to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or worried during the day, outside of when you're lying awake at night?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to separate it, honestly. During the day I’m busy enough that I don’t always think, “Oh, I’m anxious,” but I’m definitely tense a lot. It’s like I’m constantly trying to keep everything from slipping, especially at work. I’ll be looking at one set of figures and already worrying about the next thing, or whether I missed something, or whether someone’s going to ask a question I should’ve anticipated.\n\nSo… yes, I guess there is worry during the day, but it feels almost normal to me at this point, if that makes sense. Like it’s just the background noise of my day. I don’t always notice it until I realize my shoulders are up around my ears or I’ve been rereading the same line over and over because my mind is already ten steps ahead.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain tasks or situations because they make you feel anxious or overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… I don’t think of it as “avoiding” at first, because I tell myself I’m just prioritizing or I’ll get to it when I have a clearer head, but honestly, yes.\n\nAt work, the bigger models and presentations have become this thing I almost circle around. I’ll open the file, look at it, feel completely overwhelmed by how much could be wrong or how many assumptions someone might challenge, and then I’ll go answer emails or tweak some smaller thing that doesn’t matter as much. And then of course the important work slips, which makes everything worse. I actually got a formal performance warning recently because my models and presentations have been late or not where they need to be. That was… humiliating, honestly. I’ve always been someone people could rely on for that.\n\nAnd outside work, I’ve basically stopped showing up for things that used to keep me steady. I used to volunteer teaching, and I had yoga, and a book club. They weren’t huge things, but they made my life feel like it had something in it besides spreadsheets and deadlines. Now I avoid them because the thought of having to be “on,” or answer people asking where I’ve been, or just manage another commitment feels like too much. So I don’t go, and then I feel worse because my life has gotten very small.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any physical symptoms like headaches or stomach problems during the day, not just when waking up in the morning?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, I don’t think I’ve been tracking it that clearly. The morning stomach-tightness stands out because it’s so immediate when I wake up, so I notice that. During the day, I’m often just pushing through whatever is happening, so I don’t always stop and think, “This is a physical symptom.”\n\nI wouldn’t want to say yes in a definite way if I’m not sure. I guess I notice tension in my body sometimes, but as far as actual headaches or stomach problems during the day… I don’t know. Nothing specific is jumping out at me right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social interactions or gatherings because you feel anxious about them?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. I mean, I don’t always label it as anxiety in the moment, but that’s probably what it is. I’ve stopped going to things I used to actually care about — volunteer teaching, yoga, book club. Those were sort of the pieces of my life that made me feel like I wasn’t just work and exhaustion, you know? And now even the thought of showing up feels like too much.\n\nPart of it is worrying that people will ask where I’ve been, or that I’ll have to explain myself, or just make conversation and seem normal. So I tell myself I’m too busy or I’ll go next week, but then I don’t. And it’s not helping — my life has gotten really narrow.\n\nWork has been affected too. I’ve had a formal performance warning because my models and presentations keep slipping, and I think some of that is the same pattern — avoiding things that feel too overwhelming, then falling further behind. It’s embarrassing to say out loud, because I used to be much more reliable than this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden feeling of intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think I’ve had something that clearly fits that, like a sudden wave of terror that peaks really fast and feels like an “attack,” if that’s what you mean.\n\nI do get overwhelmed, and sometimes it can feel like everything is too much, but it’s more… it builds, or it’s tied to a situation I’m already dreading. I’m not thinking of a specific time where out of nowhere I had intense fear or discomfort that shot up within minutes. At least nothing that stands out to me that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're observing yourself from outside your body or like you're in a dream?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. Or at least… not in a clear way that I would describe like that. I know what you mean, I think — like feeling detached from yourself, or unreal, or like you’re watching yourself from somewhere else — and that’s not really something I’ve noticed.\n\nThere are times when I feel kind of numb or like I’m going through the motions, but I wouldn’t say I feel outside my body or like I’m in a dream. It’s more just… I’m there, I’m aware of what’s happening, I just may not feel very connected or engaged. But no, I can’t think of a time where I literally felt like I was observing myself from outside or that things around me weren’t real.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're disconnected from your thoughts or feelings?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I mean, I definitely feel dulled sometimes, like my reactions are slower or flatter than they should be, but I don’t know if that’s the same as being disconnected from my thoughts or feelings.\n\nMy thoughts are usually very much there — almost too much there — but feelings can be harder to access or name, if that makes sense. Like I’ll know intellectually that something should make me happy or upset, and I can talk through it logically, but emotionally it feels muted or far away. But I still feel like they’re mine. I don’t feel separate from them in the way you might mean.\n\nSo… I don’t think I’ve noticed a true disconnection, exactly. And now even the thought of showing up feels like too much.\n\nPart of it is worrying that people will ask where I’ve been, or that I’ll have to explain myself, or just make conversation and seem normal. So I tell myself I’m too busy or I’ll go next week, but then I don’t. And it’s not helping — my life has gotten really narrow.\n\nWork has been affected too. I’ve had a formal performance warning because my models and presentations keep slipping, and I think some of that is the same pattern — avoiding things that feel too overwhelming, then falling further behind. It’s embarrassing to say out loud, because I used to be much more reliable than this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden feeling of intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think I’ve had something that clearly fits that, like a sudden wave of terror that peaks really fast and feels like an “attack,” if that’s what you mean.\n\nI do get overwhelmed, and sometimes it can feel like everything is too much, but it’s more… it builds, or it’s tied to a situation I’m already dreading. I’m not thinking of a specific time where out of nowhere I had intense fear or discomfort that shot up within minutes. At least nothing that stands out to me that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're observing yourself from outside your body or like you're in a dream?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. Or at least… not in a clear way that I would describe like that. I know what you mean, I think — like feeling detached from yourself, or unreal, or like you’re watching yourself from somewhere else — and that’s not really something I’ve noticed.\n\nThere are times when I feel kind of numb or like I’m going through the motions, but I wouldn’t say I feel outside my body or like I’m in a dream. It’s more just… I’m there, I’m aware of what’s happening, I just may not feel very connected or engaged. But no, I can’t think of a time where I literally felt like I was observing myself from outside or that things around me weren’t real.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're disconnected from your thoughts or feelings?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I mean, I definitely feel dulled sometimes, like my reactions are slower or flatter than they should be, but I don’t know if that’s the same as being disconnected from my thoughts or feelings.\n\nMy thoughts are usually very much there — almost too much there — but feelings can be harder to access or name, if that makes sense. Like I’ll know intellectually that something should make me happy or upset, and I can talk through it logically, but emotionally it feels muted or far away. But I still feel like they’re mine. I don’t feel separate from them in the way you might mean.\n\nSo… I don’t think I’ve noticed a true disconnection, exactly. More like I’m tired and kind of numbed out at times, but not detached from myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any physical symptoms like fatigue during the day?\n\nPatient: Yes. Very much. I mean, that’s honestly one of the main reasons I came in — I barely sleep in any way that actually helps anymore.\n\nI’ll go to bed exhausted, but then I just lie there awake, replaying work in my head… client models, numbers, things I need to fix, conversations I had or need to have. It’s like my brain won’t shut down. And then even if I do fall asleep, I’ll wake up at 3 or 4 in the morning with my stomach tight, already feeling like I’m behind before the day has even started.\n\nSo by the time I get to work, I feel like I never really rested. I’m physically there, but I feel worn out during the day, like I’m dragging myself through it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden, intense feeling of fear or discomfort that seemed to come out of nowhere?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re describing, like a sudden wave of terror out of nowhere.\n\nI do get tense or uneasy sometimes, especially when I’m already worn down, but it usually feels tied to something I’m thinking about or dealing with. It’s not like I’m sitting there and then suddenly I’m overwhelmed by fear for no reason. I wouldn’t describe it as intense fear, exactly.\n\nMaybe discomfort, yes, but more gradual, more like it builds or sits there in the background. I don’t think I’ve had those sudden episodes where it comes on sharply and unexpectedly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a panic attack?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I have, no. At least not what I understand a panic attack to be.\n\nI’ve had periods where I feel really uncomfortable or keyed up, but it’s not like this sudden, overwhelming rush where I feel like I’m losing control or something terrible is happening. It’s more… I don’t know, a steady tension, or being worn down and uneasy. If panic attack means something very specific, maybe I’m not describing it correctly, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had one.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your physical symptoms, like the tension and fatigue, are related to your worries about work or are they something you experience separately?\n\nPatient: I think they’re very much connected to work, yes. Not separate, or at least it doesn’t feel separate to me.\n\nThe fatigue is mostly because I’m not sleeping in any useful way anymore. I’ll get into bed already exhausted, and then instead of actually resting, I’m lying there going over client models, numbers, what I may have missed, what I need to fix the next morning. It just keeps looping. And then if I do fall asleep, I often wake up around 3 or 4 in the morning with my stomach tight, like my body has already started the workday before I’ve even opened my laptop.\n\nSo by the time I’m actually at work, I feel like I never really slept. It’s not just being tired like I had a busy week — it’s more like I never got to recover in the first place.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I don’t think I’m avoiding people specifically because I’m afraid they’ll judge me, at least not in a clear, conscious way.

I mean, I can be self-conscious sometimes, like anyone, especially in professional settings where I feel like I’m supposed to have everything together. But if I pull back socially, it doesn’t usually feel like, “Oh, they’re going to evaluate me,” or “They’ll think badly of me.” It’s more that I just don’t always have the energy for it, or I don’t feel up to carrying a conversation.

So… I wouldn’t say fear of being judged is the main reason. If anything, I sometimes worry I won’t be very good company, but that feels a little different than being afraid of scrutiny.

Now I avoid them because the thought of having to be “on,” or answer people asking where I’ve been, or just manage another commitment feels like too much. So I don’t go, and then I feel worse because my life has gotten very small.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any physical symptoms like headaches or stomach problems during the day, not just when waking up in the morning?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, I don’t think I’ve been tracking it that clearly. The morning stomach-tightness stands out because it’s so immediate when I wake up, so I notice that. I guess I notice tension in my body sometimes, but as far as actual headaches or stomach problems during the day… I don’t know. Nothing specific is jumping out at me right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social interactions or gatherings because you feel anxious about them?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. I mean, I don’t always label it as anxiety in the moment, but that’s probably what it is. I’ve stopped going to things I used to actually care about — volunteer teaching, yoga, book club. Those were sort of the pieces of my life that made me feel like I wasn’t just work and exhaustion, you know? I’ve had a formal performance warning because my models and presentations keep slipping, and I think some of that is the same pattern — avoiding things that feel too overwhelming, then falling further behind. It’s embarrassing to say out loud, because I used to be much more reliable than this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden feeling of intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think I’ve had something that clearly fits that, like a sudden wave of terror that peaks really fast and feels like an “attack,” if that’s what you mean.\n\nI do get overwhelmed, and sometimes it can feel like everything is too much, but it’s more… it builds, or it’s tied to a situation I’m already dreading. I’m not thinking of a specific time where out of nowhere I had intense fear or discomfort that shot up within minutes. At least nothing that stands out to me that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're observing yourself from outside your body or like you're in a dream?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. Or at least… not in a clear way that I would describe like that. I know what you mean, I think — like feeling detached from yourself, or unreal, or like you’re watching yourself from somewhere else — and that’s not really something I’ve noticed.\n\nThere are times when I feel kind of numb or like I’m going through the motions, but I wouldn’t say I feel outside my body or like I’m in a dream. It’s more just… I’m there, I’m aware of what’s happening, I just may not feel very connected or engaged. But no, I can’t think of a time where I literally felt like I was observing myself from outside or that things around me weren’t real.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're disconnected from your thoughts or feelings?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I mean, I definitely feel dulled sometimes, like my reactions are slower or flatter than they should be, but I don’t know if that’s the same as being disconnected from my thoughts or feelings.\n\nMy thoughts are usually very much there — almost too much there — but feelings can be harder to access or name, if that makes sense. Like I’ll know intellectually that something should make me happy or upset, and I can talk through it logically, but emotionally it feels muted or far away. But I still feel like they’re mine. I don’t feel separate from them in the way you might mean.\n\nSo… I don’t think I’ve noticed a true disconnection, exactly. More like I’m tired and kind of numbed out at times, but not detached from myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any physical symptoms like fatigue during the day?\n\nPatient: Yes. Very much. I mean, that’s honestly one of the main reasons I came in — I barely sleep in any way that actually helps anymore.\n\nI’ll go to bed exhausted, but then I just lie there awake, replaying work in my head… client models, numbers, things I need to fix, conversations I had or need to have. It’s like my brain won’t shut down. And then even if I do fall asleep, I’ll wake up at 3 or 4 in the morning with my stomach tight, already feeling like I’m behind before the day has even started.\n\nSo by the time I get to work, I feel like I never really rested. I’m physically there, but I feel worn out during the day, like I’m dragging myself through it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden, intense feeling of fear or discomfort that seemed to come out of nowhere?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re describing, like a sudden wave of terror out of nowhere.\n\nI do get tense or uneasy sometimes, especially when I’m already worn down, but it usually feels tied to something I’m thinking about or dealing with. It’s not like I’m sitting there and then suddenly I’m overwhelmed by fear for no reason. I wouldn’t describe it as intense fear, exactly.\n\nMaybe discomfort, yes, but more gradual, more like it builds or sits there in the background. I don’t think I’ve had those sudden episodes where it comes on sharply and unexpectedly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a panic attack?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I have, no. At least not what I understand a panic attack to be.\n\nI’ve had periods where I feel really uncomfortable or keyed up, but it’s not like this sudden, overwhelming rush where I feel like I’m losing control or something terrible is happening. It’s more… I don’t know, a steady tension, or being worn down and uneasy. If panic attack means something very specific, maybe I’m not describing it correctly, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had one.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your physical symptoms, like the tension and fatigue, are related to your worries about work or are they something you experience separately?\n\nPatient: I think they’re very much connected to work, yes. Not separate, or at least it doesn’t feel separate to me.\n\nThe fatigue is mostly because I’m not sleeping in any useful way anymore. I’ll get into bed already exhausted, and then instead of actually resting, I’m lying there going over client models, numbers, what I may have missed, what I need to fix the next morning. It just keeps looping. And then if I do fall asleep, I often wake up around 3 or 4 in the morning with my stomach tight, like my body has already started the workday before I’ve even opened my laptop.\n\nSo by the time I’m actually at work, I feel like I never really slept. It’s not just being tired like I had a busy week — it’s more like I never got to recover in the first place.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding social interactions because you fear being judged or evaluated by others?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I don’t think I’m avoiding people specifically because I’m afraid they’ll judge me, at least not in a clear, conscious way.\n\nI mean, I can be self-conscious sometimes, like anyone, especially in professional settings where I feel like I’m supposed to have everything together. But if I pull back socially, it doesn’t usually feel like, “Oh, they’re going to evaluate me,” or “They’ll think badly of me.” It’s more that I just don’t always have the energy for it, or I don’t feel up to carrying a conversation.\n\nSo… I wouldn’t say fear of being judged is the main reason. If anything, I sometimes worry I won’t be very good company, but that feels a little different than being afraid of scrutiny.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I guess… not really, no. Or at least I don’t experience it as, “I can’t go because people will think something bad about me.”

If I avoid things, it’s usually more vague than that. Like I’ll look at a dinner or a work gathering or even just meeting someone for coffee and think, “I don’t have it in me.” I might worry a little that I’ll seem flat or distracted or not very engaging, but it’s not this strong fear that they’re judging me. It’s more that the whole thing feels effortful, and I don’t trust myself to be present.

So I wouldn’t say fear of what others think is the main driver. There might be a little self-consciousness around the edges, but not enough that I’d call it the reason I avoid people.

I mean, I definitely feel dulled sometimes, like my reactions are slower or flatter than they should be, but I don’t know if that’s the same as being disconnected from my thoughts or feelings.\n\nMy thoughts are usually very much there — almost too much there — but feelings can be harder to access or name, if that makes sense. Like I’ll know intellectually that something should make me happy or upset, and I can talk through it logically, but emotionally it feels muted or far away. But I still feel like they’re mine. I don’t feel separate from them in the way you might mean.\n\nSo… I don’t think I’ve noticed a true disconnection, exactly. More like I’m tired and kind of numbed out at times, but not detached from myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any physical symptoms like fatigue during the day?\n\nPatient: Yes. Very much. I mean, that’s honestly one of the main reasons I came in — I barely sleep in any way that actually helps anymore.\n\nI’ll go to bed exhausted, but then I just lie there awake, replaying work in my head… client models, numbers, things I need to fix, conversations I had or need to have. It’s like my brain won’t shut down. And then even if I do fall asleep, I’ll wake up at 3 or 4 in the morning with my stomach tight, already feeling like I’m behind before the day has even started.\n\nSo by the time I get to work, I feel like I never really rested. I’m physically there, but I feel worn out during the day, like I’m dragging myself through it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden, intense feeling of fear or discomfort that seemed to come out of nowhere?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re describing, like a sudden wave of terror out of nowhere.\n\nI do get tense or uneasy sometimes, especially when I’m already worn down, but it usually feels tied to something I’m thinking about or dealing with. It’s not like I’m sitting there and then suddenly I’m overwhelmed by fear for no reason. I wouldn’t describe it as intense fear, exactly.\n\nMaybe discomfort, yes, but more gradual, more like it builds or sits there in the background. I don’t think I’ve had those sudden episodes where it comes on sharply and unexpectedly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a panic attack?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I have, no. At least not what I understand a panic attack to be.\n\nI’ve had periods where I feel really uncomfortable or keyed up, but it’s not like this sudden, overwhelming rush where I feel like I’m losing control or something terrible is happening. It’s more… I don’t know, a steady tension, or being worn down and uneasy. If panic attack means something very specific, maybe I’m not describing it correctly, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had one.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your physical symptoms, like the tension and fatigue, are related to your worries about work or are they something you experience separately?\n\nPatient: I think they’re very much connected to work, yes. Not separate, or at least it doesn’t feel separate to me.\n\nThe fatigue is mostly because I’m not sleeping in any useful way anymore. I’ll get into bed already exhausted, and then instead of actually resting, I’m lying there going over client models, numbers, what I may have missed, what I need to fix the next morning. It just keeps looping. And then if I do fall asleep, I often wake up around 3 or 4 in the morning with my stomach tight, like my body has already started the workday before I’ve even opened my laptop.\n\nSo by the time I’m actually at work, I feel like I never really slept. It’s not just being tired like I had a busy week — it’s more like I never got to recover in the first place.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding social interactions because you fear being judged or evaluated by others?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I don’t think I’m avoiding people specifically because I’m afraid they’ll judge me, at least not in a clear, conscious way.\n\nI mean, I can be self-conscious sometimes, like anyone, especially in professional settings where I feel like I’m supposed to have everything together. But if I pull back socially, it doesn’t usually feel like, “Oh, they’re going to evaluate me,” or “They’ll think badly of me.” It’s more that I just don’t always have the energy for it, or I don’t feel up to carrying a conversation.\n\nSo… I wouldn’t say fear of being judged is the main reason. If anything, I sometimes worry I won’t be very good company, but that feels a little different than being afraid of scrutiny.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you avoid social situations because you're afraid of what others might think of you?\n\nPatient: I guess… not really, no. Or at least I don’t experience it as, “I can’t go because people will think something bad about me.”\n\nIf I avoid things, it’s usually more vague than that. Like I’ll look at a dinner or a work gathering or even just meeting someone for coffee and think, “I don’t have it in me.” I might worry a little that I’ll seem flat or distracted or not very engaging, but it’s not this strong fear that they’re judging me. It’s more that the whole thing feels effortful, and I don’t trust myself to be present.\n\nSo I wouldn’t say fear of what others think is the main driver. There might be a little self-consciousness around the edges, but not enough that I’d call it the reason I avoid people.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not sure I’d say “most of the time,” no. I mean, I do get anxious, but it usually feels like there’s something attached to it — some reason I can point to, even if maybe the reaction is bigger than it needs to be.

When you say “no apparent reason,” that’s where I hesitate, because I don’t usually feel anxious just out of nowhere, like sitting quietly and suddenly panicking for no reason. It’s more that my mind finds something to hook onto, or there’s already something in the background that feels unresolved. So I wouldn’t describe myself as anxious all the time without a cause.

I guess I’m often tense or on edge, but in my head there’s usually a “because.” Whether that because is reasonable is another question.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF41.1, F43.0, F45.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I’m barely sleeping in any way that actually helps. I mean, I’m in bed, technically, but I’ll lie there for hours with my mind running through client models and numbers and assumptions, like I’m still at my desk, and I can’t seem to shut it off.\n\nAnd then even when I do fall asleep, I wake up at 3 or 4 in the morning with my stomach all tight, like I’m already bracing for the day. By the time I get to work, I feel like I never really rested at all. It’s just been wearing me down, and I didn’t know what else to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or worried during the day, outside of when you're lying awake at night?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to separate it, honestly. During the day I’m busy enough that I don’t always think, “Oh, I’m anxious,” but I’m definitely tense a lot. It’s like I’m constantly trying to keep everything from slipping, especially at work. I’ll be looking at one set of figures and already worrying about the next thing, or whether I missed something, or whether someone’s going to ask a question I should’ve anticipated.\n\nSo… yes, I guess there is worry during the day, but it feels almost normal to me at this point, if that makes sense. Like it’s just the background noise of my day. I don’t always notice it until I realize my shoulders are up around my ears or I’ve been rereading the same line over and over because my mind is already ten steps ahead.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain tasks or situations because they make you feel anxious or overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… I don’t think of it as “avoiding” at first, because I tell myself I’m just prioritizing or I’ll get to it when I have a clearer head, but honestly, yes.\n\nAt work, the bigger models and presentations have become this thing I almost circle around. I’ll open the file, look at it, feel completely overwhelmed by how much could be wrong or how many assumptions someone might challenge, and then I’ll go answer emails or tweak some smaller thing that doesn’t matter as much. And then of course the important work slips, which makes everything worse. I actually got a formal performance warning recently because my models and presentations have been late or not where they need to be. That was… humiliating, honestly. I’ve always been someone people could rely on for that.\n\nAnd outside work, I’ve basically stopped showing up for things that used to keep me steady. I used to volunteer teaching, and I had yoga, and a book club. They weren’t huge things, but they made my life feel like it had something in it besides spreadsheets and deadlines. Now I avoid them because the thought of having to be “on,” or answer people asking where I’ve been, or just manage another commitment feels like too much. So I don’t go, and then I feel worse because my life has gotten very small.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any physical symptoms like headaches or stomach problems during the day, not just when waking up in the morning?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, I don’t think I’ve been tracking it that clearly. The morning stomach-tightness stands out because it’s so immediate when I wake up, so I notice that. During the day, I’m often just pushing through whatever is happening, so I don’t always stop and think, “This is a physical symptom.”\n\nI wouldn’t want to say yes in a definite way if I’m not sure. I guess I notice tension in my body sometimes, but as far as actual headaches or stomach problems during the day… I don’t know. Nothing specific is jumping out at me right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social interactions or gatherings because you feel anxious about them?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. I mean, I don’t always label it as anxiety in the moment, but that’s probably what it is. I’ve stopped going to things I used to actually care about — volunteer teaching, yoga, book club. Those were sort of the pieces of my life that made me feel like I wasn’t just work and exhaustion, you know? And now even the thought of showing up feels like too much.\n\nPart of it is worrying that people will ask where I’ve been, or that I’ll have to explain myself, or just make conversation and seem normal. So I tell myself I’m too busy or I’ll go next week, but then I don’t. And it’s not helping — my life has gotten really narrow.\n\nWork has been affected too. I’ve had a formal performance warning because my models and presentations keep slipping, and I think some of that is the same pattern — avoiding things that feel too overwhelming, then falling further behind. It’s embarrassing to say out loud, because I used to be much more reliable than this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden feeling of intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think I’ve had something that clearly fits that, like a sudden wave of terror that peaks really fast and feels like an “attack,” if that’s what you mean.\n\nI do get overwhelmed, and sometimes it can feel like everything is too much, but it’s more… it builds, or it’s tied to a situation I’m already dreading. I’m not thinking of a specific time where out of nowhere I had intense fear or discomfort that shot up within minutes. At least nothing that stands out to me that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're observing yourself from outside your body or like you're in a dream?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. Or at least… not in a clear way that I would describe like that. I know what you mean, I think — like feeling detached from yourself, or unreal, or like you’re watching yourself from somewhere else — and that’s not really something I’ve noticed.\n\nThere are times when I feel kind of numb or like I’m going through the motions, but I wouldn’t say I feel outside my body or like I’m in a dream. It’s more just… I’m there, I’m aware of what’s happening, I just may not feel very connected or engaged. But no, I can’t think of a time where I literally felt like I was observing myself from outside or that things around me weren’t real.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're disconnected from your thoughts or feelings?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I mean, I definitely feel dulled sometimes, like my reactions are slower or flatter than they should be, but I don’t know if that’s the same as being disconnected from my thoughts or feelings.\n\nMy thoughts are usually very much there — almost too much there — but feelings can be harder to access or name, if that makes sense. Like I’ll know intellectually that something should make me happy or upset, and I can talk through it logically, but emotionally it feels muted or far away. But I still feel like they’re mine. I don’t feel separate from them in the way you might mean.\n\nSo… I don’t think I’ve noticed a true disconnection, exactly. More like I’m tired and kind of numbed out at times, but not detached from myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any physical symptoms like fatigue during the day?\n\nPatient: Yes. Very much. I mean, that’s honestly one of the main reasons I came in — I barely sleep in any way that actually helps anymore.\n\nI’ll go to bed exhausted, but then I just lie there awake, replaying work in my head… client models, numbers, things I need to fix, conversations I had or need to have. It’s like my brain won’t shut down. And then even if I do fall asleep, I’ll wake up at 3 or 4 in the morning with my stomach tight, already feeling like I’m behind before the day has even started.\n\nSo by the time I get to work, I feel like I never really rested. I’m physically there, but I feel worn out during the day, like I’m dragging myself through it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden, intense feeling of fear or discomfort that seemed to come out of nowhere?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re describing, like a sudden wave of terror out of nowhere.\n\nI do get tense or uneasy sometimes, especially when I’m already worn down, but it usually feels tied to something I’m thinking about or dealing with. It’s not like I’m sitting there and then suddenly I’m overwhelmed by fear for no reason. I wouldn’t describe it as intense fear, exactly.\n\nMaybe discomfort, yes, but more gradual, more like it builds or sits there in the background. I don’t think I’ve had those sudden episodes where it comes on sharply and unexpectedly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a panic attack?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I have, no. At least not what I understand a panic attack to be.\n\nI’ve had periods where I feel really uncomfortable or keyed up, but it’s not like this sudden, overwhelming rush where I feel like I’m losing control or something terrible is happening. It’s more… I don’t know, a steady tension, or being worn down and uneasy. If panic attack means something very specific, maybe I’m not describing it correctly, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had one.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your physical symptoms, like the tension and fatigue, are related to your worries about work or are they something you experience separately?\n\nPatient: I think they’re very much connected to work, yes. Not separate, or at least it doesn’t feel separate to me.\n\nThe fatigue is mostly because I’m not sleeping in any useful way anymore. I’ll get into bed already exhausted, and then instead of actually resting, I’m lying there going over client models, numbers, what I may have missed, what I need to fix the next morning. It just keeps looping. And then if I do fall asleep, I often wake up around 3 or 4 in the morning with my stomach tight, like my body has already started the workday before I’ve even opened my laptop.\n\nSo by the time I’m actually at work, I feel like I never really slept. It’s not just being tired like I had a busy week — it’s more like I never got to recover in the first place.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding social interactions because you fear being judged or evaluated by others?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I don’t think I’m avoiding people specifically because I’m afraid they’ll judge me, at least not in a clear, conscious way.\n\nI mean, I can be self-conscious sometimes, like anyone, especially in professional settings where I feel like I’m supposed to have everything together. But if I pull back socially, it doesn’t usually feel like, “Oh, they’re going to evaluate me,” or “They’ll think badly of me.” It’s more that I just don’t always have the energy for it, or I don’t feel up to carrying a conversation.\n\nSo… I wouldn’t say fear of being judged is the main reason. If anything, I sometimes worry I won’t be very good company, but that feels a little different than being afraid of scrutiny.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you avoid social situations because you're afraid of what others might think of you?\n\nPatient: I guess… not really, no. Or at least I don’t experience it as, “I can’t go because people will think something bad about me.”\n\nIf I avoid things, it’s usually more vague than that. Like I’ll look at a dinner or a work gathering or even just meeting someone for coffee and think, “I don’t have it in me.” I might worry a little that I’ll seem flat or distracted or not very engaging, but it’s not this strong fear that they’re judging me. It’s more that the whole thing feels effortful, and I don’t trust myself to be present.\n\nSo I wouldn’t say fear of what others think is the main driver. There might be a little self-consciousness around the edges, but not enough that I’d call it the reason I avoid people.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious most of the time, even when there's no apparent reason to be anxious?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “most of the time,” no. I mean, I do get anxious, but it usually feels like there’s something attached to it — some reason I can point to, even if maybe the reaction is bigger than it needs to be.\n\nWhen you say “no apparent reason,” that’s where I hesitate, because I don’t usually feel anxious just out of nowhere, like sitting quietly and suddenly panicking for no reason. It’s more that my mind finds something to hook onto, or there’s already something in the background that feels unresolved. So I wouldn’t describe myself as anxious all the time without a cause.\n\nI guess I’m often tense or on edge, but in my head there’s usually a “because.” Whether that because is reasonable is another question.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

---------- turn 16 | patient:alignment | patient.build_alignment_messages() | model=gpt-5.5 ----------
========== INPUT [patient] ==========
[

It’s more that my mind finds something to hook onto, or there’s already something in the background that feels unresolved. I’m not totally sure how to answer that, because I don’t think I track it that clearly in the moment.

If I’m alone, I can feel uneasy sometimes, but I don’t always know whether I’d call it anxiety exactly. It’s not like I’m sitting there thinking someone is judging me, obviously, because no one’s there. And if I’m doing something I normally enjoy, I can still feel kind of unsettled or distracted, but again, I don’t know if that’s anxiety or just… not being able to fully relax.

So I guess yes, I can feel tense or uncomfortable even when there’s no evaluation happening, but I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed a clear pattern where I’m anxious in all those situations. It’s more blurry than that for me.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I’m barely sleeping in any way that actually helps. I mean, I’m in bed, technically, but I’ll lie there for hours with my mind running through client models and numbers and assumptions, like I’m still at my desk, and I can’t seem to shut it off.\n\nAnd then even when I do fall asleep, I wake up at 3 or 4 in the morning with my stomach all tight, like I’m already bracing for the day. By the time I get to work, I feel like I never really rested at all. It’s just been wearing me down, and I didn’t know what else to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or worried during the day, outside of when you're lying awake at night?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to separate it, honestly. During the day I’m busy enough that I don’t always think, “Oh, I’m anxious,” but I’m definitely tense a lot. It’s like I’m constantly trying to keep everything from slipping, especially at work. I’ll be looking at one set of figures and already worrying about the next thing, or whether I missed something, or whether someone’s going to ask a question I should’ve anticipated.\n\nSo… yes, I guess there is worry during the day, but it feels almost normal to me at this point, if that makes sense. Like it’s just the background noise of my day. I don’t always notice it until I realize my shoulders are up around my ears or I’ve been rereading the same line over and over because my mind is already ten steps ahead.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain tasks or situations because they make you feel anxious or overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… I don’t think of it as “avoiding” at first, because I tell myself I’m just prioritizing or I’ll get to it when I have a clearer head, but honestly, yes.\n\nAt work, the bigger models and presentations have become this thing I almost circle around. I’ll open the file, look at it, feel completely overwhelmed by how much could be wrong or how many assumptions someone might challenge, and then I’ll go answer emails or tweak some smaller thing that doesn’t matter as much. And then of course the important work slips, which makes everything worse. I actually got a formal performance warning recently because my models and presentations have been late or not where they need to be. That was… humiliating, honestly. I’ve always been someone people could rely on for that.\n\nAnd outside work, I’ve basically stopped showing up for things that used to keep me steady. I used to volunteer teaching, and I had yoga, and a book club. They weren’t huge things, but they made my life feel like it had something in it besides spreadsheets and deadlines. Now I avoid them because the thought of having to be “on,” or answer people asking where I’ve been, or just manage another commitment feels like too much. So I don’t go, and then I feel worse because my life has gotten very small.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any physical symptoms like headaches or stomach problems during the day, not just when waking up in the morning?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, I don’t think I’ve been tracking it that clearly. The morning stomach-tightness stands out because it’s so immediate when I wake up, so I notice that. During the day, I’m often just pushing through whatever is happening, so I don’t always stop and think, “This is a physical symptom.”\n\nI wouldn’t want to say yes in a definite way if I’m not sure. I guess I notice tension in my body sometimes, but as far as actual headaches or stomach problems during the day… I don’t know. Nothing specific is jumping out at me right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social interactions or gatherings because you feel anxious about them?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. I mean, I don’t always label it as anxiety in the moment, but that’s probably what it is. I’ve stopped going to things I used to actually care about — volunteer teaching, yoga, book club. Those were sort of the pieces of my life that made me feel like I wasn’t just work and exhaustion, you know? And now even the thought of showing up feels like too much.\n\nPart of it is worrying that people will ask where I’ve been, or that I’ll have to explain myself, or just make conversation and seem normal. So I tell myself I’m too busy or I’ll go next week, but then I don’t. And it’s not helping — my life has gotten really narrow.\n\nWork has been affected too. I’ve had a formal performance warning because my models and presentations keep slipping, and I think some of that is the same pattern — avoiding things that feel too overwhelming, then falling further behind. It’s embarrassing to say out loud, because I used to be much more reliable than this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden feeling of intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think I’ve had something that clearly fits that, like a sudden wave of terror that peaks really fast and feels like an “attack,” if that’s what you mean.\n\nI do get overwhelmed, and sometimes it can feel like everything is too much, but it’s more… it builds, or it’s tied to a situation I’m already dreading. I’m not thinking of a specific time where out of nowhere I had intense fear or discomfort that shot up within minutes. At least nothing that stands out to me that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're observing yourself from outside your body or like you're in a dream?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. Or at least… not in a clear way that I would describe like that. I know what you mean, I think — like feeling detached from yourself, or unreal, or like you’re watching yourself from somewhere else — and that’s not really something I’ve noticed.\n\nThere are times when I feel kind of numb or like I’m going through the motions, but I wouldn’t say I feel outside my body or like I’m in a dream. It’s more just… I’m there, I’m aware of what’s happening, I just may not feel very connected or engaged. But no, I can’t think of a time where I literally felt like I was observing myself from outside or that things around me weren’t real.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're disconnected from your thoughts or feelings?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I mean, I definitely feel dulled sometimes, like my reactions are slower or flatter than they should be, but I don’t know if that’s the same as being disconnected from my thoughts or feelings.\n\nMy thoughts are usually very much there — almost too much there — but feelings can be harder to access or name, if that makes sense. Like I’ll know intellectually that something should make me happy or upset, and I can talk through it logically, but emotionally it feels muted or far away. But I still feel like they’re mine. I don’t feel separate from them in the way you might mean.\n\nSo… I don’t think I’ve noticed a true disconnection, exactly. More like I’m tired and kind of numbed out at times, but not detached from myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any physical symptoms like fatigue during the day?\n\nPatient: Yes. Very much. I mean, that’s honestly one of the main reasons I came in — I barely sleep in any way that actually helps anymore.\n\nI’ll go to bed exhausted, but then I just lie there awake, replaying work in my head… client models, numbers, things I need to fix, conversations I had or need to have. It’s like my brain won’t shut down. And then even if I do fall asleep, I’ll wake up at 3 or 4 in the morning with my stomach tight, already feeling like I’m behind before the day has even started.\n\nSo by the time I get to work, I feel like I never really rested. I’m physically there, but I feel worn out during the day, like I’m dragging myself through it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden, intense feeling of fear or discomfort that seemed to come out of nowhere?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re describing, like a sudden wave of terror out of nowhere.\n\nI do get tense or uneasy sometimes, especially when I’m already worn down, but it usually feels tied to something I’m thinking about or dealing with. It’s not like I’m sitting there and then suddenly I’m overwhelmed by fear for no reason. I wouldn’t describe it as intense fear, exactly.\n\nMaybe discomfort, yes, but more gradual, more like it builds or sits there in the background. I don’t think I’ve had those sudden episodes where it comes on sharply and unexpectedly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a panic attack?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I have, no. At least not what I understand a panic attack to be.\n\nI’ve had periods where I feel really uncomfortable or keyed up, but it’s not like this sudden, overwhelming rush where I feel like I’m losing control or something terrible is happening. It’s more… I don’t know, a steady tension, or being worn down and uneasy. If panic attack means something very specific, maybe I’m not describing it correctly, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had one.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your physical symptoms, like the tension and fatigue, are related to your worries about work or are they something you experience separately?\n\nPatient: I think they’re very much connected to work, yes. Not separate, or at least it doesn’t feel separate to me.\n\nThe fatigue is mostly because I’m not sleeping in any useful way anymore. I’ll get into bed already exhausted, and then instead of actually resting, I’m lying there going over client models, numbers, what I may have missed, what I need to fix the next morning. It just keeps looping. And then if I do fall asleep, I often wake up around 3 or 4 in the morning with my stomach tight, like my body has already started the workday before I’ve even opened my laptop.\n\nSo by the time I’m actually at work, I feel like I never really slept. It’s not just being tired like I had a busy week — it’s more like I never got to recover in the first place.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding social interactions because you fear being judged or evaluated by others?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way.
